[[The Gathered Rise Up]] Resolution of the Polish Sejm on commemorating the memory of Stanisław Mikołajczyk on the 50th anniversary of death On 13 December 2016, the 50th anniversary of the death of Stanisław Mikołajczyk, statesman, leader of the Polish People's Party, Prime Minister of the Polish Government in exile passed. Stanisław Mikołajczyk belongs to the group of Polish politicians for whom free, independent, just and democratic Poland was the highest good. With his life and his work, he gave this unmistakable evidence. In the years 1918-1919 he participated in the Wielkopolska Uprising, and in 1920 as a volunteer he participated in defending his homeland during the Polish-Bolshevik War. In 1937, he led the Great Boy Strike against the authoritarian government of sanation. He also fought for independence in 1939. As Prime Minister of the Polish Government in London, he sought Poland's rightful place among the free, democratic peoples of Europe, not always finding understanding and support among his own politicians and allies. After the war he returned to the country and headed the Polish People's Party, which sought to build a state based on social justice and governed by the authorities elected in democratic elections. Full of faith in the better future of Poland became part of the Provisional Government of National Unity, recognized by the Allies as a representation of the nation. This government was intended to combine different political environments for the reconstruction of the country. Mikołajczyk lost the fight for democracy with communist totalitarianism. Forced to leave for exile he continued to fight for free Poland. He was always faithful to his beliefs. On the 50th anniversary of the death of Stanisław Mikołajczyk, the Sejm of the Republic of Poland pays tribute to the leader of the folk movement and defender of democracy. I ask Members to adopt a resolution by acclamation. [[Applause]] Thank you very much. I state that the Sejm adopted a resolution on honoring the memory of Stanisław Mikołajczyk on the 50th anniversary of his death. Formal proposals have been made by Members. Member Krzysztof Gadowski, Civic Platform Club.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u30	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a report by the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the draft law amending the Prison Service Act and some other laws, prints 1063 and 1111. This government submission envisages entrusting the Prison Service with tasks related to ensuring security and order in the Ministry of Justice and in the National Public Prosecutor's Office. The special nature of the tasks performed by the Minister for Justice of the Prosecutor-General may involve the occurrence of threats to the security of the office serving the Minister for Justice acting simultaneously as the Prosecutor-General. Such a level of protection is not ensured by entities carrying out tasks under the Act of 22 August 1997 on the protection of persons and property. It is appropriate to supplement the legal order with provisions providing for the assignment of tasks in this area to the Prison Service as a formation with both knowledge and experience in ensuring the protection of criminal establishments and detentions against the activities of the criminal world, including in particular organised criminal groups. The scope of the tasks will be decided by the Minister of Justice as head of the Prison Service, taking into account the fact that both the office serving the Minister of Justice and the National Public Prosecutor's Office are located in several locations. In order to carry out the tasks set out in the Prison Service Act of 9 April 2010, it was necessary to amend the Law of 24 May 2013 on measures of direct coercion and firearms, and in particular Article. Article 35 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 point 10 determining the cases in which a Prison Service officer is entitled to decide to use or use direct coercion measures only in the event of an attempted direct, unlawful attempt on the life or health of a entitled person or other person, an attempt to escape by a deprived person, and a pursuit of him or a person who has carried out a direct unlawful attack on the facilities of a prison, an investigation or other organisational unit in which the Prison Service ensures order and security. There is also a revision of the Article. 40 § 2 of the Law of 28 January 2016 on the Public Prosecutor's Office, which is the consequence of the amendments made to the Prison Service Act and aims to ensure the security of the Public Prosecutor's Office. This is carried out by entities carrying out tasks pursuant to the Act on the Protection of Persons and Property with the provisions laid down to enable the National Public Prosecutor's Office to carry out those tasks. This change will be financed by the funds which the National Public Prosecutor's Office spends on the protection of its premises as part of the purchase of personal and property protection services. The envisaged date of entry into force of the Act is the day after the date of publication. This is dictated by the expiry date of the current agreement for the protection of the District Attorney's Office... excuse me, the National Public Prosecutor's Office on 31 December 2016. In favour of shortening the vacathio legis, there is an important public interest in ensuring the security of the Minister of Justice for the Prosecutor General and the staff of the Public Prosecutor's Office as of 1 January 2017 by the Prison Service officers. The Committee on Justice and Human Rights, after first reading and considering this draft law at its meeting on 6 December 2016, requests the High Sejm to adopt a draft law. Thank you very much.

The programme includes in particular the purchase of weapons, the purchase of special equipment and equipment, the purchase and modernisation of technical protection equipment and fire-fighting equipment, the thermomodernisation of construction facilities, the provision of electricity security of penitentiary units, the improvement of the technical condition of construction infrastructure, the purchase and replacement of rolling stock, the modernisation of medicinal entities for persons deprived of freedom, the modification of the system of employment of medical personnel. I would like to stress here that there are about 100 doctors missing from the Prison Service and that appropriate conditions should be created for employment under civil law agreements. It's a matter of payback for the prison service officers and the prison staff. These issues are also adequately secured in the state budget for 2017 and 2020 and even in the longer term until 2026. The amount from the state budget in the first period up to 2020 will be 1 520 265 thousand, and is set out in the Act and in the annex to the Act for individual years, and by 2026 and 2 917 269 thousand. The Minister of Justice will supervise the implementation of this programme. It should be stressed that the Prison Service is the only formation in a country that has not been modernised since 1989. It's an armed formation. The programme also plans to purchase semi-automatic carbines, grenade launchers, which is related to the 2015 withdrawal programme for other weapons, Kbk AKMS and RWGL, from the Prison Service equipment, due to the need to switch service to weapons with less force of destruction. It is also planned to purchase protective kits in the amount of more than 1000 pieces, protective helmets - over 1500 pieces, bulletproof vests for a total amount of 2,200 thousand. It is also planned to purchase devices for the detection of narcotic drugs, devices for the detection of mobile phones. In order to ensure the electrical safety of penitentiary units, 51 generating units must be installed or replaced. It is planned to liquidate the Penalty Facility in Opole and build a new one in the vicinity of Opole with a capacity of about 650 places for prisoners. The investment cost was set at 250 million. An increase in the salary of officers is planned from 1 January 2017, with an increase in the multiple of the basic amount, representing the average salary of officers from 3.01 to 3.19, an average of PLN 250 will increase the salary from 1 January 2017. There will also be an increase in salaries for civil servants. It should be stressed that there are over 27 000 officers and that there are more than 1800 civil servants. There are over 70,000 prisoners in prisons. The Law and Justice Club supports this project as a project necessary for the modernisation of the Prison Service in Poland. Thank you very much. The law so, while receding a bit in history, the modernisation and reform activities of the Prison Service during more than a quarter of a century of the existence of free Poland were very intense in my opinion. I am referring here to the first years after 1989, when Paweł Moczydłowski led the Central Board of Penitentiary Works at the Ministry of Justice by reforming both the Prison Service and the treatment of prisoners, improving the living conditions in prisons. In my opinion, such a milestone was also the Prison Service Act of 9 April 2010 - a legal act that comprehensively regulated matters related to the functioning of the Prison Service; a law that adapted the Prison Service to the standards in force in other uniform services, as well as the requirements of the Constitution and European Union law. In recent years, the formation was also gradually equipped with new equipment, the construction of penal facilities - such an example is the Department of Penalty in Opole Lubelski - ensuring the proper conditions for the execution of the penalty, and for civil servants and employees providing better working conditions. Other examples include the construction of a production and storage facility in Opolskie Strzelce or the thermomodernization of the building of the penitentiary. These are examples of investments that ended, it seems, in 2015. As I said, the process of modernising the Prison Service continues. We see the positive aspect of the project presented, but its implementation depends on securing funds in the budget of the State for the next years of its operation, and that is not the case, and this raises our doubts. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u86	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Poselski Club on the Government's draft law establishing the Police Modernization Programme, the Border Guard, the State Fire Department and the Government Protection Office in the years 2017-2020 and published in print No 1062, following the presentation of the report of the committee in print No 1089. Ladies and gentlemen, the bill submitted to the High House concerns the establishment of a modernisation programme aimed at improving the effectiveness and efficiency of the police, the Border Guard, the State Fire Department and the Government Protection Office. According to the provisions of the Act, as a result of the implementation of the projects which are part of the programme, conditions will be created to facilitate the implementation of the tasks of the formations mentioned by me. The main tasks of the programme are to modernise the infrastructure, equipment and equipment, as well as to strengthen the incentive system of civil servants' salaries and increase the competitiveness of the remuneration of the members of the formation. The government submission envisages spending over 4 years on the total amount of over PLN 9186 million from the state budget. This amount consists of the financial resources included in the budgets of the various formations, i.e. over PLN 5470 million, as well as external measures, which, according to the justification presented by the government, will be included in the corresponding budget provisions for the years 2017 and 2020 in the corresponding special-purpose reserve and will amount to more than PLN 3715 million. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u178	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present our position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Code of Civil Procedure Act on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party. Today's amendment concerns an acceptable time limit for reopening civil proceedings. This is an exceptional remedy which allows for the removal from legal trading of defective final judgments terminating proceedings in a substantive manner. This measure undermines the stability of final judgments in civil matters and is therefore limited to the most serious deficiencies that have occurred in the course of the proceedings. The request to reopen the proceedings may be based, inter alia, on a false document, the presence of an unauthorised person in the court or the lack of adequate representation of the party during the proceedings. The draft under discussion today results from the need to comply with the judgment of the Constitutional Court of 22 September 2015. In that judgment, the Constitutional Tribunal held that the five-year pre-emptive period, the expiry of which ultimately closes the judicial route to reopen the proceedings, infringes the Article. Article 77 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2nd Constitution of the Republic of Poland. So far, there have been situations where the European Court of Human Rights, after lengthy proceedings, has opened its way to bringing an action for reopening proceedings, but due to a time limit exceeding 5 years such a request could not be taken into account. This problem was noted by the Constitutional Tribunal, recognising the Article. 408 The Civil Procedure Code, which 5 years after the judgment was passed, does not allow for the renewal of proceedings, except for a few exceptions, to be incompatible with the Constitution. At the same time, the Court stressed that there cannot be a situation where, despite the rightness of the citizen's complaint, he has no opportunity to re-examine the case. The new solution adopted is intended to ensure effective legal protection for all citizens affected by judicial authorities. Project in Art. The following shall be added to the Code of Civil Procedure: 408. After the expiry of ten years, the judgment may not be renewed, except where the party has been prevented from acting or has not been duly represented. The proposed amendment will facilitate the exercise of its rights by citizens who have turned their affairs over to the European Court of Human Rights. The ECHR's proceedings can last for many years, hence the five-year period for the possibility of reopening judicial proceedings seems to be far from sufficient. This threatens a situation in which the citizen, holding the ECHR ruling in his hand, will in practice not be able to implement it. The proposed period of 10 years will fully safeguard the interests of those who have brought matters to the judgment of the ECHR in Strasbourg and all others who are looking for the possibility of reopening their cases. The solution in which this 10-year period applies not only to cases dealt with by the Court in Strasbourg, but also to all cases where the 5-year period currently applies. First, the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal is implemented in this way. Secondly, this solution will prevent situations where the time limit provided for in Article. 408 Kpc may be shorter than the time limit for the submission of damages claims - 3 years after the injured party finds out about the damage and the person liable to remedy it, but not more than 10 years after the date on which the injurious event occurred. The proposed solution takes into account the need to balance both the principle of protection of acquired rights and procedural justice and the principle of binding international law and the right to court. It shall also take into account the value of the validity of judgments. On the one hand, it will guarantee citizens the right to resume civil proceedings within a reasonable and realistic time, and on the other hand it will safeguard the stability of civil law relations established on the basis of final judgments of civil courts. Ladies and gentlemen, on behalf of the club PSL request that the project be submitted for further work.

As a result of the work of the subcommittee and commission, the final shape of the bill, which is in print no. 1125, has been developed. In the course of the work on the records of the various amendments proposed by the government in Draft Print Act No 795, the amendments tabled by the Members were considered. Some of these amendments were linguistic and logistical in nature. They were discussed, discussed and adopted in a joint vote. Several substantive amendments considered take into account the comments made in the opinion of the Sejm Analysis Office. The purpose of these amendments is to seal the records to remove interpretation doubts. These amendments are further clarified, inter alia. records relating to e.g. a gaming directory subject to the scope of the Gambling Act. The government bill sets out the necessary conditions for the organisation of games covered by the state monopoly. A monopolist shall be required to have the rules of procedure approved by the Minister for Finance. The amendment adopted by the Finance Committee clarifies the time limit within which the Minister responsible for Public Finance is required to approve these Rules of Procedure. Let me remind you that one of the objectives of the changes proposed by the government is to protect gambling players, especially minors. Another amendment adopted by the committee obliges the entity performing the state monopoly to implement the rules of the relevant game in which the verification procedure will be concluded for the end of the 18th year of age of the participants in the game, but in this regulation there should also be a record of the procedure for the verification of the age of persons entering the gaming room. This amendment will improve the effectiveness of the supervision of state authorities over slot machines in arcades. The committee members also accepted an amendment which included the archiving of all data related to gambling, i.e. those games that are organised via the Internet. In the next amendment, the Commission made it clear that the entity performing the State monopoly on numbers games and money lotteries also has the possibility to organise multi-jurisdictional games. In the light of this solution, an amendment was also adopted allowing the State monopoly company to entrust its activities with the organisation of such games to the organiser of the multi-jurisdictional game. The Act introduces an obligation to register lottery equipment. The standardization introduced by one of the amendments also allows the withdrawal of such registration. The possibility of withdrawal has been introduced in justified cases. The withdrawal may be made by the Minister responsible for public finances on reasonable grounds. The Minister responsible for public finances may also withdraw the authorisation granted for technical research. The Commission, in line with, inter alia, the need to protect society, including minors, and in particular the need to protect against the negative effects of gambling, has introduced an amendment limiting the scope of advertising. Further amendments also state that the way of limitation and the whole system of restrictions, the mode of limitation of these advertisements is to be laid down in a regulation issued by the competent minister. This amendment sets the record straight. Since criminal liability is specific to a natural person, the wording of this amendment is therefore formulated in such a way as to make it clear who can be burdened with such penalties. There is also clarification of the provisions that would prevent the establishment of a tax base for online games. Rules have been introduced to lay down criminal sanctions in respect of those violating the monopoly. In subsequent amendments, the committee introduced rules allowing the body imposing the penalty to determine its amount, taking into account the circumstances, the reasons which would have led to the imposition of the penalty are set out in the catalogue described. The Commission has also accepted amendments which respond to the European Commission's comments made to the draft law. One of these changes is to change the requirement to use Polish language in speech and writing on the possibility of communicating in Polish to the extent necessary for the performance of the duties of the representative. This record has been liberalised, softened. Recalling the main principles of the bill, it should be noted that the main objective of the bill is to eliminate the so-called. a gray area for games on slots and games on the Internet. The project generally extends significantly the scope of gambling and includes gambling by state monopoly. The extension of the offer consists of introducing numbers games and money lottery into the Internet, arranging games on slots not only in casino games, but also in arcades on slots and offering online gambling. The project also assumes liberalisation of the rules of poker and allows you to organize casino games via the Internet, performed by a state company. Similarly, a state monopoly is introduced in slot arcades. The project also includes the problem of protecting players from the negative effects of gambling, as well as raising the level of public awareness of risks, and we consider this to be a very important problem. To this end, it is possible to block access to websites offering illegal gambling, to prohibit payment services from being made available by providers of these services to illegal operators, and thus to hinder payment. All applicants for a licence or a permit to operate will be required to draw up and apply the rules of procedure of the responsible game to be approved by the Minister of Finance. He covers in detail who, when, on what terms... I'm not going to talk about those details anymore, but it's a very important document. The project does not change taxes related to gambling. Neither the tax rates nor the form of their settlement are changed, while it is the extension of the catalogue of entities subject to monetary penalty and a significant increase in the amount of penalties for illegal operators. The penalties will not only be imposed on operators who organise gambling in breach of a licence or a permit, but also on those who own the premises, those who violate the rules on blocking websites, payment agents, and those who perform managerial functions in operators who organise gambling without a licence. In conclusion, we believe that the gambling market needs to be organized. The current level of state control over the gambling market is insufficient and we do not see a contraindication for gambling to be covered by the state monopoly. A very important problem to pay particular attention to is the issue of addiction, the protection of players from the negative effects of gambling, and the raising of public awareness of these risks. And on these issues, we are looking forward to a decisive improvement in the situation. We hope that the state monopoly on gambling will not only be fiscal, but above all preventive and protective against its negative effects. That's why we're going to vote. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u210	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation and the Committee on Foreign Affairs, I have the honour to present a report on the Government's draft law on ratification of the European Agreement on major inland waterways of international importance, hereinafter: AGN, drawn up in Geneva on 19 January 1996. The intention of the agreement is to develop international transport on inland waterways in Europe, which is low cost compared to other land transport modes and has a number of environmental advantages, starting with a reduction in the adverse environmental impact of the transport sector. In particular, it aims to establish the legal framework necessary to establish a coordinated development plan, to build a network of inland roads of international importance on the basis of agreed infrastructure parameters. Their network, contained in the AGN agreement, is divided into nine main water transport routes of 27 000. km that connects ports of more than 37 European countries. I do not hide that ratification is very expected, because schools in Poland are still training sailors, but unfortunately they do not have a job, they cannot get a job in Poland and have to work with a Western company. One of such schools is the Inland Navigation Technique in Nakle nad Notecy, which I am a graduate of. And that's why I keep in touch with the school all the time. There, they are deeply affected by this ratification. In addition, in my previous term of office, I had the opportunity to chair the parliamentary group for the regeneration of the Vistula. As part of this team we had specialists who spoke about the importance of the Vistula River, but also the entire network of rivers in Poland, as well as specialists who carried out expansion, revitalization in the Czech Republic. The company Spelwar from Tricity performed very large works there. The Baltic daily also runs a water road on the Vistula. So it is a highly valued ratification also in Pomerania, where ports develop in such a period of about 10% per year. And Vistula or Odra are very desirable rivers, because they can further develop these ports. In the medium and long term, the development of infrastructure for inland waterway transport will benefit both economically, financially and socially and environmentally. The economic effects of the agreement are divided into several categories. In a strategic dimension for the Polish economy, the main beneficiary of the development of inland transport will be the dynamically developing largest seaports in which it will be, in addition to road and rail transport, an integral mode of transport providing an efficient connection to the facilities. In Poland, as we know, the TEN-T network includes roads: the E30 linking the Baltic Sea with the Danube in Bratislava, including the Odra River in Poland from Świnoujście to the border with the Czech Republic, and the E40 binding the Baltic Sea with the Black Sea - the route leads from Gdansk first to Vistula, Narwa, Bug to Brest, and then to Dnieprem in the Chernobyl region, formerly through Kiev, New Kachowka to Kherson. The third route is the E70. It leads from Holland to Russia, to Lithuania. In Poland it runs the Oder from the Hawel channel to the mouth of Warta in Kostrzyn, via the water Vistula - Odra, to Bydgoszcz lower Vistula, through the Vistula to Szkarpawa, through the mouth Nogatu and the Vistula Bay. The Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Maritime Affairs has planned, prepared a project on the development of inland waterways for the years 2016-2020 and with a view to 2030. I'm not hiding that the costs are very high, but we have to start somewhere. The important thing is that there are plans that are to reactivate AGN. The content of the AGN agreement is in line with the legal provisions and will not require changes in the national legal order at this stage, and therefore the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation and the Committee on Foreign Affairs jointly recommend the adoption by the High Sejm of a draft law on ratification of the European Agreement on major inland waterways of international importance contained in print No 1015. There is also the opinion of BAS, which says that this is in line with the Constitution. Ratification, as far as the Ministry of Foreign Affairs is concerned, is going well. Thank you very much.

Social issues. According to analyses prepared by the UN, waterways in Poland, except for short sections on the lower Oder and lower Vistula, do not meet the minimum international conditions for navigability. In this context the UN's agreement on major inland waterways of international importance, also called the AGN Convention or the AGN Agreement, cannot be recalled. In principle, this act creates a legal framework to facilitate the coordination of plans for the development and investment of existing international inland waterway transport. The main aim is to ensure that transport by inland waterways in Europe is even more efficient and attractive to both users and operators. It is worth adding that the agreement sets out the technical parameters to be met by international inland waterways. Main targets. In its findings, the AGN refers to the network of the European waterways system within the trans-European transport network - roads marked with the symbol E. It also sets out priority investment projects, which, taking into account the requirements of modern technologies, are intended to lead to the elimination of the so-called. narrow throats, i.e. these elements of roads E, whose parameters do not meet the current requirements of international waterways, i.e. they have a class lower than IV, so-called. basic bottlenecks, or although they do not meet the requirements of Class IV, should be modernised in order to improve their quality and allow the volume of transport to increase, so-called. strategic bottlenecks, the elimination of missing links consisting of the formation of currently non-existent but part of a future network of international waterways. AGN Convention and Poland. In relation to Polish waterways, the following qualifications and types are included in the recommendations resulting from the AGN agreement: strategic narrow throat - river Odra from Widuchowa to Szczecin, basic narrow throat - river Odra from Koźl to Widuchowa and the Gliwicki Canal, river Wisła: from Warsaw to Płock and from Włocławek to Gdańsk, river Bug: from Brest to Lake Zegrzynski, Canal Żerański: from Lake Zegrzynski to Wisła [[♪ The bell ♪]] and missing connections: channel Odra - Danube - Elbe. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u252	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft report presented relates to the draft law, which responds to the situation in prisons, namely the decline in prisoners' employment. Some doubts were raised. the scale of deductions from the prison pay, up to 45%, to the fund that is used to return the entrepreneur's funds, above all to this - it is also the construction of halls, also other things, but to a large extent it is an incentive for entrepreneurs, it is a reimbursement of costs. It has to be taken into account that, of course, the pay, the prisoner's wages fall then, but at the same time the prisoner has the free cost of living in prison, so that in total it does not even return these maintenance costs or compensate in some way these maintenance costs, and that these low wages, which are effectively - what he gets on his hand - below the minimum wage, is not only a phenomenon in us, but it is quite a common phenomenon elsewhere. Some doubts were raised as to how it would be implemented, how entrepreneurs would be invited to those halls that would arise, whether they would be tenders or competitions. For now, there is no problem, because there is no crowd of entrepreneurs who would volunteer, but such a problem may someday arise in practice. In fact, we believe that this bill is needed. Much will depend on the way it is done, but it offers a chance to stop this negative trend when it comes to the percentage of prisoners employed. And we accept the Ministry's assurances that the Ministry will primarily make efforts to look for opportunities and expand opportunities for paid work for prisoners, and that this is, I would say, the second possibility, which is a complementary possibility. The Civic Platform will continue to support this project once the Minister has clarified all these doubts and we will also follow how it will be implemented.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u258	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Members' Club of the Polish People's Party, I will present our position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Penal Code Act. This proposal will return to the proceedings of the High House following its adoption as unchanged by the Committee on Justice and Human Rights. Its main goal is to increase employment among people imprisoned in penitentiary units in Poland. In accordance with the current wording of the implementing Penal Code for ordering and auxiliary work carried out for the organisation of the Prison Service or for ordering work for the benefit of the local government, up to a maximum of 90 hours per month, no remuneration shall be payable to the sentenced person. After the planned amendment, this provision is to be supplemented, inter alia. about work for social purposes for entities for which the authority of the municipality, district or voivodship is the body of the founding, state or local organisational units and companies governed by commercial law with the exclusive participation of the State Treasury, the municipality, the district or the voivodship. Social work will also be possible for institutions or organisations representing the local community and educational institutions, youth educational centres, youth sociotherapy centres, medicinal entities within the meaning of the provisions on medical activities, organizational units of social assistance, foundations, associations and other institutions or organisations of general interest providing charitable assistance. This means no less than an extension of the number of entities for which prisoners can carry out work free of charge. It is also worth mentioning here the Supreme Court's opinion on this project, in which the proposed change can contribute to increasing the opportunities for inmates to take up work. In turn, the Helsinki Foundation for Human Rights estimates that the proposal represents a step towards the reversal of the Code rule, according to which inmates' employment is paid. Here we should ask: Shouldn't we also look for solutions to increase the ability of prisoners to work for pay instead of just expanding the number of persons entitled to work for unpaid work? Let me remind you that at present only 40% Inmates shall carry out works of a paid nature. An attempt to respond to this challenge is to increase the financial capacity of the Convicted Professional Activation Fund and the Industrial Development of Labor Works by increasing from 25 to 45% deductions for this purpose from the remuneration of the convicted person. At the same time from 10 to 7% the reduction of deductions from this remuneration for the harmed and Post-Penitentiary Assistance Fund. Released in this way 3% remuneration is intended to be used for the professional activation of persons deprived of freedom. This solution should encourage businesses to employ convicted persons in a wider way than before. The additional funds of the fund are also intended to be used for the construction of production facilities in the penitentiary units, which should be considered as a favourable solution. Doubts about reducing prison pay should dispel international statistics, which are also worth paying attention to. Poland as one of the few countries currently offers 100% of residents minimum wage for work, while in France and the UK it is 44%, while in Sweden and Hungary it is about 1/3 of the minimum wage. A higher deduction to the Professional Activation Fund will leave prisoners' wages to a higher level than in the countries concerned. In our opinion, the projects discussed today will increase the number of prisoners working for the benefit of themselves, as well as those entitled to work in prison. The project should also have a positive impact on the costs of maintaining the penitentiary system in Poland. Therefore, the Polish People's Party's parliament club is in favour of proceding this law, on this project further. I think that, as a result, it will also support the adoption of this law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u276	Dear Marshal! Dear Minister, High Izbo! I have the honour to submit to the High House a report on the teamwork of the two Commissions: Infrastructure and the Territorial Government and Regional Policy. They held a joint meeting on 30 November, working on a government draft law contained in Seym Printing 1013, which concerns amendments to the following laws: in the Law of 21 March 1985 on public roads, in the Law of 16 December 2005 on the financing of land transport infrastructure and in the Law of 27 August 2009 on public finances. The proposed rules aim to eliminate doubts about the possibility of financial cooperation between local government units and other entities, including. Treasury, in the field of financing road investments. Proposals for amendments to the Article 19 Public Roads Act is intended to clearly indicate that it is possible to terminate and conclude agreements between road managers in order to implement and finance investments which, for various reasons, are interested in local government units located in road lanes of categories other than their own. Thus, the proposed amendments to the Transport Infrastructure Financing Act clearly clarify the possibility for local governments to transfer funds to other road managers. These changes are in line with the demands of many local authorities, who want to spend money on road tasks managed by other institutions in the interests of the safety of their residents and their communication needs, e.g. by the General Director of National Roads and Motorways. Designed provision in Article. Article 19 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 The 4th Public Road Act aims to eliminate the question of interpretation regarding the possibility of transferring road management between public road managers. According to such an agreement, both the tasks as a whole and the tasks individually can be transferred. Doubts about funding possibilities e.g. the construction of sidewalks in the national road lane by the municipalities notify regional clearing houses, assuming that the provisions of the Municipal Government Act of 8 March 1990 and the Public Finance Act of 27 August 2009 do not provide for the possibility for municipalities to grant grants to governmental organisations and bodies. The current ambiguous rules limit the possibility for local authorities to transfer public funds to infrastructure investments located in the road lane of another road manager. Additional justification for explicit solutions is situations where local authorities are required to improve road safety e.g. in the municipality area and having public resources, they ask other public road managers to enable the implementation of e.g. walking-bike or additional road safety devices. Despite the appropriateness of implementing these projects, due to the imprecise rules in this area, they are not implemented due to interpretation doubts as to the legal basis for the financing by the municipality of elements of road infrastructure, district roads, voivodships or national roads. This leads to a situation in which the local government, with financial resources to carry out the task, cannot achieve it due to doubts about the existence of legal bases. In view of the doubts raised by the Regional Audit Chamber, it is therefore necessary to clarify the rules so as to give the local authorities a clear basis for the transfer of funds e.g. for the construction of pavements or bicycle paths on roads of a different category than those managed by them. The proposed rules therefore respond to the demands made by the local authorities regarding clear regulations indicating the possibility to implement investments that are relevant to the needs of local communities. At the same time, due to the doubts raised by local government units regarding the absence of appropriate amendments to the Public Finance Act, taking into account the purpose of the proposed regulation, the draft law provides in Article. 3 in the field of Article. 216 Public Finance Act adding paragraph. 3, which clearly indicates that expenditure on the budget of a local government unit may be used to carry out the tasks arising from the agreements referred to in Article. Article 19 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 of the Law of 21 March 1985 on public roads, i.e. agreements concluded between road managers. On the other hand, the amendment proposed in the Article. Point 1 (1) of the draft law concerns the introduction of a new provision under which the Minister responsible for Transport will be able to issue, disseminate or recommend standards and standards for the preparation of investments in public roads, construction, reconstruction, repair, maintenance or protection of such roads. In the current Article. Article 17 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Public Road Act defines the competence of the Minister responsible for transport as the body headed by the road administration responsible for the organisation, development and management of the public road network. According to Art. Article 17 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 Point 1 (2) of the Public Road Act belongs to the Minister responsible for transport, among others. issuing technical and operational regulations for road and road engineering facilities. However, the need to introduce the possibility for the Minister responsible for transport also to issue standards and standards relating to the preparation of investments in public roads, construction, reconstruction, renovation, maintenance or protection of these roads is reported. Thus, the Minister will be able to issue recommendations in the form of standards and standards relating to, for example, for the design, construction and maintenance of public roads or road engineering facilities. In this way, it is possible, for example. rapid implementation of new technologies that allow very often for cost-effective and environmentally-friendly road infrastructure design, in line with the latest technical knowledge and solutions used in road and bridge construction. These models or standards will not constitute universal law, but will be suggestions. Developed by the Minister responsible for transport standards or standards in the form of e.g. Codes of good practice can facilitate and help to harmonise the practice of road managers. It should also be borne in mind that the application of these models or standards will be a matter for the individual decision of the entity that will be interested in them. Mrs. Marshal! After the first reading and recognition of the project, which took place on 30 November, as I mentioned earlier, the project was adopted without amendments. Commissions: Local Government and Regional Policy and Infrastructure unanimously request the adoption of Print Act No 1013. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u286	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Law on public roads and certain other laws. The draft law submitted is the result of the demands of local government units regarding the formulation of clear rules that would enable investment to be carried out concerning, among others, local authorities. improve the safety and functionality of public roads which are not on the management board of a given local government unit and which serve the needs of local communities. The main problem solved by the proposed law is the unambiguous definition of the possibility of agreements between road managers in order to implement investments which are interested in local government units located in road lanes other than their own. The project assumes that local authorities will be able to finance such investments without fear of a negative interpretation of expenditure by regional audit chambers. As a self-government officer with a long-term internship in municipal infrastructure of medium-sized cities, I know very well the problems to remove this bill. Simple investments consisting of construction of a bus bay, lighting of pedestrian crossings, construction or renovation of the pavement currently cannot be carried out by local government units on its own territory on roads which are on the management board of other entities. Often these are the main roads in a given village. This applies especially to small towns and villages, where there are no bypasses, and the passageways lead through their center. Indirect efforts to deliver investments for local communities are inefficient and go on for years. For example, in the case of roads managed by the General Directorate for National Roads and Motorways, it is necessary to place investments in budget plans, to raise money for their implementation, to select a contractor. The municipalities often participate in the costs of such tasks, which requires the prior signing of an agreement with the road manager. All of this greatly prolongs the achievement of such tasks. Most often, it is not possible to carry out minor tasks within the financial and organisational scope of the local government unit at all if more investments are not planned in connection with the modernisation of the entire section of the road. In addition, with such a long and irregular investment cycle, it is not possible to coordinate them with local investments, which could help to better manage public funds. The way in which such investments are financed should also be considered. A good solution could be a mandatory transfer of money corresponding to even a part of the value of a task defined, e.g. percentage that would come from the road manager's budget, e.g. from the budget of the General Directorate for National Roads and Motorways, to local self-government units. The idea is that road managers should not shift the financial burden of carrying out small but necessary tasks to local authorities, while at the same time getting rid of their statutory duties. The contested bill has another dimension, perhaps a bit humorous, but important from the point of view of the interests of local communities. In some cases local authorities have even avoided the most socially desirable investments, e.g. from better lighting transitions for pedestrians or construction of bike paths. After the entry into force of this law, they will no longer be able to say that it is impossible, because it is not our way. Of course there will be no coercion, it will depend on the operationality of a given uncle or mayor. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The bill is a good step towards simplifying procedures. It is a step towards a local and civil state. The Free and Solidarni Circle will support the bill submitted. Thank you very much.

The purpose of the revision of the Accounting Act of 29 September 1994 is to implement the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 22 October 2014. As the deadline for the implementation of the Directive with regard to disclosure of non-financial information and information relating to diversity policy by certain large entities and groups was 6 December 2016, the Public Finance Commission immediately proceeded to proceed with the procedure of that law. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u303	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Lech Sprawka, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u304	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to present a position on the government draft law amending the Accounting Act from print no. 1045. As the rapporteur has just mentioned, the draft law implements the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 22 October 2014, which amended Council Directive 2013/34/EU as regards disclosure of non-financial information and information on diversity by certain large entities and groups. It is worth recalling that this Directive introduces two major changes which, firstly, extend the scope of non-financial information and, secondly, introduce new disclosures in the field of diversity policy. As regards the first amendment, which is implemented precisely by the Government's draft law, it is primarily the introduction of a requirement to disclose in the activity report the form of a declaration on non-financial information the minimum relevant information on at least social, labour, environmental, respect for human rights and the fight against corruption and bribery, so-called. reporting on corporate social responsibility, CSR reports. On the other hand, as regards the second amendment, new disclosures on diversity policy will be consumed by amending the Finance Minister's regulation on current and periodic information published by issuers of securities and the conditions for recognising as equivalent information required by the laws of a non-member state. As I mentioned, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice fully supports the government draft law amending the Accounting Act, does not make any amendments at second reading and asks for the rapid completion of the work on this print project no. 1045. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u305	Thank you very much, sir. Mrs Bożena Szydłowska and Mr Paweł Kobyliński made club statements in writing. Please, Mrs Genowefa Tokarska, the Polish People's Party.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u306	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! As regards the committee's report on the government project, prints 1045 and 1086, as the rapporteur presented, the first reading took place at the meeting of the Committee on Public Finance. The purpose of the amendment to the Accounting Act is to implement Directive 2014/95/EU of 22 October 2014 as regards the disclosure of non-financial information and information on diversity by certain large entities. The deadline for implementing the Directive into Polish law was 6 December. As regards the changes introduced by the Directive, they, firstly, broaden the scope of information for these non-financial ones and, secondly, introduce new disclosures in the field of diversity policy. As far as the first is concerned, disclosure is required in the activity report in the form of a statement of non-financial information - a minimum of relevant information on at least social, labour, environmental, respect for human rights and the fight against corruption and bribery, i.e. the so-called. reporting on corporate responsibility as CSR reports. These disclosures must take the form of a description of the policy pursued in the field concerned, its results, a description of risks, a way of managing risks in non-financial matters. They are covered by these regulations by large individuals of interest so-called. public, i.e. those whose average annual employment is above 500 people and the balance sheet assets amount to more than 20 million or net revenue from sales of goods and products is above 40 million. The approach to implementing the Directive on disclosure of non-financial information will be based on the current state of reporting of non-financial information by Polish entities. Reporting on non-financial information will be completely voluntary and will be based on individual decisions of individual entities. The designers, the government, ensure that no additional requirements are introduced in the draft law beyond what is required by the Directive. The catalogue of units for the accounting law concerned has also been specified. They're listed in detail. There's a lot of them. The bill introduces extensive reporting requirements, as I said, for the largest large units. The project takes a flexible approach. Companies that are currently preparing e.g. CSR reports will be able to continue this form of reporting. In addition to the changes related to the implementation of the Directive, it is proposed to introduce a change which allows the transfer of certain accounting evidence from paper to IT. A new model is being introduced for this report, including for institutions, public benefit and voluntary activities. I would also like to say that the project has been subject to numerous consultations with the institutions concerned. Some of these comments have been taken into account. In conclusion, I would like to say that the bill fulfils the obligation to implement EU law, and the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party will vote in accordance with the recommendation of the Committee on Public Finance. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Chairman of the proceedings includes Deputy Marshal Stanisław Tyszka of the Sejm]]

Urz. EU L 330, 15.11.2014 p. One, late. zm.), referred to as Directive 2014/95/EU. The deadline for implementing its provisions in the Polish legal order expires on 6 December 2016. The European Union authorities have adopted Directive 2014/95/EU. Directive 2014/95/EU introduces changes resulting in: extending the scope of non-financial information and introducing new disclosures in the field of diversity policy. The Act concerns the following companies: average annual employment of more than 500 people and total balance sheet assets of more than EUR 20 million or net revenue from sales of goods and products of more than EUR 40 million. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u313	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Club on the government bill amending the Accounting Act, print no. 1045. The purpose of the amendment to the Accounting Act of 29 September 1994 is to implement Directive 2014/95/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 22 October 2014 amending Directive 2013/34/EU as regards disclosure of non-financial information and information on diversity by certain large entities and groups, called Directive 2014/95/EU. The idea of the law is to increase the transparency of social and environmental information in the area of corporate social responsibility and to improve their coherence and comparability. The changes introduced include: extending the scope of non-financial information, as well as introducing new disclosures in the field of diversity policy, which will have a significant impact on the transparency of business. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u315	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal, Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I took part in the honour of presenting to the High House a draft law on the National Cows of the Hundredth Anniversary of Recovering Independence of the Republic of Poland, contained in the sejm print No. 1093. This is the President's legislative initiative. In less than 2 years, in 2018, we will celebrate events related to the century of regaining independence by Poland. After 123 years of captivity imposed by the invaders, our country regained its desired freedom. The basis for bringing the bill to the Sejm exactly on the eve of the recently celebrated holiday of independence was the conviction of the President that the 100th anniversary of regaining independence in 2018 is of particular importance for the Polish state and the Polish nation and requires special honor. The building of regaining independence is also a great opportunity to take action to strengthen the national identity and the sense of the civic community of Poles. The President's proposal aims to prepare the National Cows of the Hundredth Anniversary of Recovering Independence of the Republic of Poland, which would take place in the years 2017 to 2021. Such a way of carrying out ceremonial celebrations would mark in the memory of Poles events and people connected with the rebirth of the Polish state. It is no less important that the President, when bringing the bill to the Sejm, expressed his conviction which I hope will be shared by all Members that there is a need to prepare the celebration in such a way that Poles can celebrate this special time together, beyond divisions, that the century of regaining independence will unite us in the sense of community and become a joyful feast of Poles and their homeland, the independent Republic of Poland. In order to realize the idea of a joint celebration, the President's proposal is to present the preparation and conduct of national celebrations in the hands of a committee composed, first, of the highest authorities of the Republic of Poland, including the President of the Republic of Poland, the Marshal of the Sejm, the Marshal of the Senate, the President of the Council of Ministers and the Government's representative for the celebrations of the century of regaining independence, and the President of the Institute of National Remembrance - the Commission for the prosecution of Crime against the Polish National. But not only should the state authorities take part in the preparation of the event. Participation in the public life of political parties and groups is no less important. The draft law discussed today assumes, of course, after agreement, participation in the committee of representatives of both political parties or groups which form parliamentary clubs in the Sejm, as well as those which are not represented in the current term of office in the Sejm, but who, in connection with the elections to the Sejm held on 25 October 2015, are entitled to a subsidy from the state budget. The political parties which have been granted the right to grant, in the form of an electoral coalition, will be given the opportunity to designate together one representative as a member of the committee. The third group of actors, whose participation will contribute to the proper organisation and conduct of the National Cows of the Hundredth Anniversary of Independence Recoveries, forms a social side. As proposed by the President, there will be representatives of churches and other religious unions, trade unions, combatants' organisations and other social organizations, or people particularly worthy of the Polish State. High Izbo! It cannot be overlooked that the National Celebrations of the Hundredth Anniversary of Independence Recovery will be a major organizational and programme undertaking. The National Independence Day is celebrated on 11 November to commemorate the return from Magdeburg to Warsaw by Józef Piłsudski in 1918. However, the process of regaining and subsequently consolidating independence was extremely complex and extended over time. Therefore, in the opinion of the President, one of the main tasks of the Committee should be to prepare a plan for the national celebrations of regaining independence each year, in the years 2017/12021. Thus, the choice of specific events and persons to be honoured in the framework of national celebrations in each year will be made by means of an autonomous decision of the Committee. In view of the organisational issues, it should be stressed here that the proposed solutions also involve the Government's representative committee on the celebration of the century of independence, acting as the Secretary of the Committee. The representative's participation will serve to ensure efficient and proper cooperation and coordination between the government centre and other actors involved in the implementation of national celebrations. It is worth noting that the proposed solution takes into account in the Committee's tasks the initiation of cooperation between state authorities, local government, social organisations and all other stakeholders interested in participation in the National Celebrations of the 100th Anniversary of Recovering Independence of the Republic of Poland. Thus, the bill presented to the High House seeks to respect and take into account the bottom-up initiatives of celebrating the century of regaining independence of citizens, local governments and other non-governmental organisations aimed at commemorating regional and local events and heroes from the period of regaining independence. It should also be pointed out that the draft contains detailed procedural rules on how to convene and organise meetings of the Committee, the conditions for participating in its work and the preservation of meetings in the form of minutes. I would like to point out here that, in accordance with the proposed provisions, the Committee's decisions will take the form of resolutions, in an open vote, by a simple majority in the presence of at least half of the members of the Committee. High Izbo! I would like to ask representatives of all parliamentary clubs to give a positive assessment of the project submitted, to direct it to further work and to adopt it as soon as possible. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u332	Thank you. Mr Ireneusz Gains, Frees and Solidarni have the floor. [[Oh, my God.]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u333	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the proposal presented by the President of the Republic of Poland for the Act on the National Obstetrics of the Hundredth Anniversary of the Recovering of Independence of the Republic of Poland. As we can read in the explanatory memorandum, the bill assumes the creation of the National Committee of the Cows of the Hundredth Anniversary of the Reclaim of Independence of the Republic of Poland, composed of the highest authorities of the Republic of Poland, representatives of political parties and groups and social organizations. The establishment of the committee aims to guarantee the celebration of the 100th anniversary of Poland's regaining independence of the highest possible rank in order to commemorate this extremely important anniversary of events from 1918, as well as events from subsequent years, during which independence was recorded throughout the entire area of the Polish Republic, such as the Polish-Bolshevik War, the Wielkopolska Uprising or the three Silesian Uprisings. The importance of organising the 100th anniversary of regaining independence by the Republic, in which representatives of all political groups, social organisations and state and local authorities would take part over political divisions, is borne out by the conclusions of the study of the National Centre for Culture conducted by TNS Polska, which was included in the presentation of the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage presented by Minister Jarosław Sellin at the meeting of the Parliamentary Group for Dignity, Community and Inclusion in 2018 of the 100th anniversary of Polish Independence Recovery which took place on 1 December 2016. As a participant in this meeting, I would like to express my appreciation for the authors of this programme, judging it as an impressive and inspiring sense of national pride. By the way, Members of the Free and Solidarni Circle have joined the envoy. the team, declaring their personal participation in the preparation of the 100th anniversary of Poland's regaining independence. The most important conclusion in our assessment, mentioned in the presentation rather by accident as the first, is: In Poland there is a feeling of a strong community. I recommend the work of the parliamentary team on the celebration of the century of independence, with which I had the opportunity to meet several days ago and present these studies in detail. They are really very interesting and they also show what is the social need for celebrating such an important holiday. Incidentally, here someone said that November 11 is the most important national holiday of Poles. In fact, these studies show that. Poles asked directly, which day in their history of the 20th century is considered the most important day, point to 11 November. But the interesting thing is that on the second place they point to May 3rd, and on the third place - here is a surprise, at least for me - August 1st, not August 15th, but August 1st, which shows the importance they attach to this break of freedom, which was the outbreak of the Warsaw Uprising in 1944. I would like to inform you that, apart from these studies, we have already conducted a competition for the visual identification of the celebration of the Independent, i.e. we have the logo of the celebration of the Independent, the anniversary of independence. This logo is simply the word "Independant" written by Józef Piłsudski himself in one of his letters. This logo will be used under the programme under different circumstances during the various events that will be initiated during these 5 years, both at home and abroad. I would also like to inform you that representatives of the President's law firm participate in the work of the interministerial team on the multi-annual government programme, for which I would like to thank, because here the synergy of the most important state institutions is of course important. I'm sure it'll be one of the events we're going to want to honor. It is worth considering and trying to achieve this in these years. We have wonderful monographs in the Sejm Publishing House and multi-volume history of the Polish Sejm, but we do not have a museum of the history of the Polish Sejm, so it is also worth taking this initiative. Within the framework of the multiannual government programme, there will be three components. It will be a program aimed at various central events. We already have a list of about 40 different events that we're going to want to finance with money specifically dedicated to this long-term government program. There will also be grants. A grant programme will be announced for local and regional events organised by various actors at local or regional level. There will also be an international component implemented to a large extent by the Adam Mickiewicz Institute, which is our specialized institution which promotes Polish culture abroad, so we want to go with promotion of historical knowledge about our fate, about our independence very, very widely. There are also concrete financial proposals in the President's bill, and we may make some comments on the division of responsibilities between the President's Registry and the Minister for Culture and National Heritage, but we generally believe that the President's proposed law fits very well into what we are also working with the government in terms of the multiannual government programme - a programme that will be finally adopted in February and will simply start functioning. We'll work out some financial matters, I think, while we're working on this bill. It is certainly also worth defining in this act the role of government representative as coordinator of the celebrations by the Council of Ministers and perhaps to supplement the provisions of the Act on the joint visual identification of these celebrations, which, as I said, we have chosen in the competition. Marshal of the Sejm on 10 November directed him to the first reading at the Sejm meeting and on 29 November the Sejm directed the bill to the Committee on Education, Science and Youth. During the meeting of the Committee on Education, Science and Youth on 14 December this year. requests have been made to reject the parliamentary draft law which did not receive the approval of the committee. Four amendments by the Modern Club and six amendments by the Law and Justice Club were tabled at this committee meeting. The Commission has taken a negative view of the four amendments tabled by the Modern Club, while it has welcomed the amendments tabled by the Law and Justice Club. The Commission asks the High Sejm to reject the amendments of the Modern Club and to accept the amendments tabled by the Law and Justice Club. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u374	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Lidia Burzyńska, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u375	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice regarding the parliamentary draft law amending the Law on Research Institutes, print No. 995. The draft law concerns the amendment of the selected provisions of the Law on Research Institutes in order to strengthen the supervision exercised by the competent minister. And here, ladies and gentlemen, I'm going to start with the encyclopedic presentation of the term: research institute. The research institute within the meaning of the Law of 30 April 2010 on research institutes is a state-owned organizational unit separated in legal, organisational, economic and financial terms, which conducts research and development work aimed at their implementation and application in practice. Ladies and gentlemen, the report of the Supreme Audit Chamber in 2015 clearly showed that supervision was the weakest link in the functioning of research institutes, hence the need for a law that will strengthen effectively and improve the supervision of the relevant minister. The audited institutes, as the report presents, did not fully exploit their research potential. Implementation activities, which are their statutory obligation, have been carried out marginally by institutes. And although the number of patented inventions doubled, this did not translate into a corresponding increase in their use. Only 20% of these have been put into practice. However, in countries with a high level of development, less than 50% of the patents obtained are considered ineffective. The Supreme Audit Chamber also pointed out that only 5% patents acquired were foreign patents, which are particularly important for the economy. The current regulations prevent this project, among others. introduces, as I said earlier, the strengthening of the supervision exercised by the competent minister - to carry out effective supervision of research institutes where the Director does not perform his duties properly. The draft law presented to the Members of Parliament offers more opportunities to make the right choice of those who have the most important positions in the institution. The same applies to the appointment of deputies, and it should be noted here that the proposed solutions provide a greater guarantee of the highest quality of management. The project also adds new regulations which concern the examination of the legality and statute of the Institute of resolutions of the pedagogical council, sorry, scientific council. The composition of this Scientific Council, as well as these regulations, are intended to ensure real, effective supervision of research institutes, because it is in the light of law that it is responsible for the functioning and effectiveness of the activities. The project has a positive socio-economic impact, has no financial impact and does not provide for public expenditure. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the Parliamentary bill amending the Law on Research Institutes. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The Act makes it clear that the Minister does not have to take into account the opinion of the Scientific Council of the Institute, which the Director wishes to appoint. At the same time, it was foreseen that the Supervisory Minister may not appoint a Director when none of the candidates submitted by the committee guarantees the proper performance of the Institute's tasks. What does that mean? The bill doesn't specify that. Theoretically, the situation when the Minister designates the so-called. its man, is a temporary - temporary situation - because after six months he should appoint a director in the competition, but the minister can always reject the candidate, and the pretext can be general, for example. that it does not guarantee the implementation of the tasks of the Institute - the specifics of course are lacking. Interestingly, to make it even more sure, three people come from the Minister on the competition committee, and only two define the scientific community. Could this mean an attempt to stubborn Polish science? Possibly, as we read into opinions about this project, we face further concerns from scientists. It is about these scientific councils, where most of the composition according to the project is also to be influenced by the Minister. According to trade union scientists, the blockade of the scientific community for decisions made in research institutes can affect their scientific results, and so, as we read in the opinion of "Solidarity," they see the future of research institutes in black colors. Ladies and gentlemen, we know that white is white and black is black - we do not agree to black scenarios for Polish science, so we will not support this project. The PSL Club will be opposed to the changes made. Thank you very much.

After the miraculous defense of Jasna Góra during the Swedish flood - on 1 April 1656 King Jan Kazimierz, taking his vows in the Lviv Cathedral, recommended to the Republic of Poland the care and defence of Our Lady, which he chose as Queen of Poland. We had to wait until 1717 for the royal vow to be fulfilled. The decision of the Holy See allowed the coronation of Madonna as the first papal tiaras from outside Rome and the use of the title of Queen of the Polish Crown. The coronation ceremony united all states and was a nationwide manifestation of faith. The act recognising Mary Queen of Poland has become a reference point for national uprisings, the formation of the idea of freedom and the hope of spiritual rebirth. The image of God parents on Jasna Gora - honored for more than 600 years - is one of the most important religious and material national treasures. Jasna Góra as the spiritual capital of Poland is the main center of this cult and pilgrimage on Polish soil. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland, convinced of the special importance of Marian worship for our Homeland not only in religious, but also in social, patriotic and cultural aspects, establishes the year 2017 as the year of the 300th anniversary Coronation of the Picture of Our Lady of Czestochowa. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u416	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear three minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Anna Sobecka, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u417	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Club of Law and Justice to present to you a position justifying the establishment of the year 2017 of the coronation of Our Lady of Czestochowa, Queen of the Polish Crown, on the 300th anniversary of this event. On 8 September 1717 the Holy See's decision for the first time outside Rome allowed the coronation of the papal tiaras and the use of the title of Queen of the Polish Crown. This ceremony was similar to the coronation of the kings of Poland and was a complement to the act of King John Kazimierz, who, after the miraculous defense of Jasna Góra during the Swedish Deluge in the Cathedral of Lviv, elected the Most Holy Virgin Queen and Patron of the Kingdom of Poland. Since 8 September 1717. Mary eventually became the ruler of Poland. Over the centuries, generations of Poles worshipped her as their queen, entrusted her to themselves and to our nation. It is worth noting that the coronation of Mary in 1717 was not only a ecclesiastical event, but also a state event. According to historical sources, the ceremony of the coronation united in the preparation and coronation of King Augustus II, Members and Senators of the Republic, clergy, nobles and was a national manifestation of faith. The coronation of the image of the Blessed Virgin Mary is primarily a manifestation of the sonly gratitude and love of God's people towards Mary, who is surrounded by peoples and nations by her motherly care. It is also a call to gratitude for God's favor and blessing received through it, which so many of Mary's worshipers receive. Putting the crown on the image depicting the Blessed Mother with the Child Jesus on her hand means the highest worship, honor and homage of the one who is the Mother of God-Man, Jesus Christ, who is the Immaculate and Queen of the world. In this way the faithful speak and worship Mary. It is also important to highlight the achievements and great importance of Jasna Góra as an important place of our history and an important center of religious worship.
