ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u427	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Culture and Media on the draft resolution presented by the Sejm Bureau on the celebration of the 75th anniversary of the death of the Polish Marshal Edward Śmigłego-Rydz, prints no. 1095 and 1123. The Committee on Culture and Media at its meeting on 13 December 2016 discussed the above draft resolution. After the discussion, the committee introduced several substantive and stylistic amendments to the title of the resolution and to the content of both paragraphs. Then, in the vote, 13 votes against, 1 against and 2 abstentions gave a positive opinion on the draft resolution commemorating the Marshal of Poland. The above draft resolution, drawn up by the Sejm Bureau following the amendments of the Committee on Culture and Media, is as follows: The resolution of the Polish Sejm on honoring the Polish Marshal Edward Śmigłego-Rydz on the 75th anniversary of his death. On 2 December 1941, Edward Śmigły-Rydz - a patriot, activist of independence organizations, prominent legionary commander and hero of the Polish-Bolshevik war in 1920. Edward Śmigły-Rydz in independent Poland was an army inspector, after the death of Józef Piłsudski - General Inspector of the Armed Forces, and on 10 November 1936 he was appointed Marshal of Poland. During the war of 1939, he served as Chief Leader. On the night of September 17 to 18, he crossed the Romanian border and was interned. [[Voice from the room: He's gone.]] In October 1941, he returned to Poland, where he soon died. He was buried in the Powęzkowski cemetery as Adam Zawisza. On the 75th anniversary of the death of Marshal Edward Śmigłego-Rydz, the Sejm of the Republic of Poland pays tribute to His achievements from the period of the struggle for independence and borders of the Homeland and honors His memory. So much for the draft resolution. It is worth referring to the fact that the discussion raised the issue of various assessments of the 1930s, when first the general, then the Marshal Edward Śmigły-Rydz had a very big influence on the state affairs. There were also various assessments of his behaviour during the September 1939 campaign, including critical assessments of his behaviour. It should be noted, however, that the conclusion of the draft resolution clearly indicates that the Polish Sejm primarily pays tribute to its achievements in the field of independence struggles and borders of the country. The Committee on Culture and Media shared the conviction of the Sejm Bureau that due to these merits for Poland's independence, because they are so large, it is necessary to celebrate the Marshal of Śmigły-Rydz with a commemorative resolution. I think it is worth recalling this period of his life, however, to illustrate more strongly what his merit was. Edward Rydz was born in Brzeżany on 11 March 1886. He was a graduate of the Academy of Fine Arts, an artist, which is not meaningless, I think, for the Committee on Culture and Media. In 1908, he joined the Union of Action and later joined the Union of Shooting. Then he accepted the nickname "Blade." Then he was the commandant of the Strzelecki Union in Lviv and editor of the monthly magazine "Sagittarius." During World War I he fought in the Legions of Polish Józef Piłsudski. As Commander 1. The infantry regiment distinguished itself among others. in the battle of the Konars. He was interim commander of the Legion Brigade I. In 1917 he became the commandant of the Polish Military Organization, anti-Russian conspiracy, but also anti-German conspiracy, which is often forgotten, also expanded on the eastern lands of the former Republic. On the threshold of independence, he was promoted to general in November 1918. He became the commander of the districts general, first Lublin, then Warsaw. In early 1919, he fought against the Ukrainians in Volyn, later as commander of the First Division of the Legion Infantry against the Bolsheviks in the north. On April 21, together with Belina-Prazhmowski's cavalry, he won Vilnius. In turn, as a result of the winter operation with the Latvians on 21 February 1920, he liberated Dyneburg and gave freedom to Latvia. Again in the south as commander of the 3rd. On 7 May the army won Kiev, which gave the opportunity to attempt to build independent Ukraine, to create a block of states between the sea, the concept of Marshal Józef Piłsudski. In the context of the liberation of Latvia, later fighting in Ukraine his military activities as a contractor of the concept of Marshal Józef Piłsudski should be emphasized. He commanded the south-east front at the time of the retreat as a result of the Bolshevik counteroffensive. Finally, as Commander 2. The army and then the central front on 16 August, being at the side of the chief chief Józef Piłsudski, led a victorious offensive from the Wieprzów region during the Battle of Warsaw. In September 1920 his 2. The army played a decisive role in a great victory over Bolsheviks in the German Battle, which eventually crashed the Soviet invasion of Poland. For the Bolshevik War General Edward Śmigły-Rydz was awarded the Cross of the Order of Virtuti Militari II class. On behalf of the Committee on Culture and Media, I recommend that the High House adopt a resolution on honoring the Polish Marshal Edward Śmigłego-Rydz. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Rydz-Śmigły, then promoted in November 1936 to the rank of Marshal of Poland. A painter by education, a soldier with a fondness connected himself with the independence movement during the First War. Piłsudski, in his opinion in his time Rydz-Śmigłego, emphasized his power of character and will, which placed him at the highest among Polish generals. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u877	Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present a governmental bill on the avoidance of double taxation and the prevention of tax evasion in the field of income taxes between the Republic of Poland and the territory to which tax law belonging to the jurisdiction of the Minister of Finance of Taiwan applies. This law introduces an agreement that will pave the way for mutual investment by introducing rules typical for international agreements on the avoidance of double taxation according to OECD standards. The agreement, which is annexed to the proposed law, is drawn up in accordance with modern standards in both the avoidance of double taxation and the safeguards limiting the use of the agreement to fully avoid taxation on each of the Contracting Parties. I'm sorry, not countries. The complicated international situation, Taiwan's international legal situation makes it impossible for us to adopt the ordinary method of ratification of the international agreement, we must apply the legal procedure. This is the mode used by a number of other countries in relation to Taiwan and constitutes a standard in this respect. I would also like to stress that a number of countries from our region, including the Visegrad Group, have such agreements signed and introduced into their legal order, which causes those countries to benefit from the higher levels of Taiwan's investment in their countries, which we cannot boast about at the moment against the background of the countries of the region. Therefore, the government's position clearly indicates the need for such a solution on the round anniversary of the 20th anniversary of the year in which Taiwan first proposed to sign such an agreement. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u878	Thank you, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. As the first Mr Jerzy Gosievski on behalf of the Law and Justice Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u879	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Dear Minister, On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the draft of the Government's Tax Avoidance Act No 1151 and to prevent tax evasion in respect of income taxes between the Republic of Poland and the territory to which tax law belonging to the jurisdiction of the Minister of Finance of Taiwan applies. The proposed solutions will form the basis for cooperation between the Republic of Poland and the territory to which tax law belonging to the jurisdiction of Taiwan's Finance Minister in the field of income taxes applies. The lack of regulation on the avoidance of double taxation in Poland's relations with Taiwan is so far an obstacle to the development of mutual economic relations. Taiwan is an important trading and investment partner. In view of the principles of Polish foreign policy in the case of Taiwan, it was not possible to conclude an act regulating the avoidance of double taxation in the form of an international agreement. On 21 October 2016, an agreement was signed between the Warsaw Trade Office in Taipei and the Taipei Economic and Cultural Office in Warsaw. The delegations which have concluded this agreement shall have specific competence, including the promotion of economic, commercial and cultural relations between Taiwan and Poland. The agreement concluded is not a classic international agreement. The main objective of the proposed law is to create a legal framework on the basis of which the principles of mutual cooperation in the field of income taxation will be defined by transposing the provisions of the agreement. The agreement uses a method of accounting proportionate to all categories of income as one of the methods of avoiding double taxation. As regards the exchange of information in tax matters, the proposed law sets international standards for the exchange of tax information between different tax administrations. The provisions on the exchange of tax information adopted in the agreement guarantee the exchange of information which may be of relevance to the administration and application of internal tax legislation, which includes an agreement. The solutions put forward by the High House introducing rules on the avoidance of double taxation in Poland's relations with Taiwan remove the obstacle to the development of mutual economic, commercial and cultural relations. Therefore, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice fully supports the draft Law on the avoidance of double taxation contained in print No 1151 and the prevention of tax evasion on income taxes between the Republic of Poland and the territory to which tax law belonging to the jurisdiction of the Minister of Finance of Taiwan applies. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The project grants students of social schools run by Polish educational organizations, parents' associations, Polish parishes and Polish sections organized at international schools and registered at the Centre for the Development of Polish Education abroad the right to relief for public transport journeys and the right to relief in the event of entry to museums and national parks in Poland. The privileges relating to travel reliefs shall also be granted to teachers in these schools. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u927	Statement on the 123rd anniversary of Mieczysław Smorawinski's birth. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 25 December this year. is the 123rd anniversary of the birth of the Polish Army Brigadier General Mieczysław Smorawinski who was murdered in Katyn. I'd like to take this opportunity to say a few words about this patriot's life. Mieczysław Makary Smorawinski was born on December 25, 1893 in Kalisz. He graduated from general school and Polish School of Commerce. When he was 18 years old, he was sentenced to six months in prison for independence and sent into Russia. After being punished, he began his studies in Lviv, during which he received military training and completed an officer's course. Smorawinski stopped his studies due to the outbreak of World War I. He went to Cracow and joined the Polish Legions, where he served as commander of the platoon, company and then battalion. After the oath crisis, Smorawinski stayed in 2. The Infantry Regiment of the Polish Food Corps and then served in the Polish Armed Forces. In 1918 he joined the Polish Army, where he became commander of Battalion II 8. The Legion Infantry regiment. He led it on the Polish-Ukrainian front, in Lithuania and Belarus. Before the end of the war, he was Commander 4. Legion Infantry Regiment and Commander of the IV Legion Infantry Brigade, which fought in the Warsaw and Hrubieszow area. After the end of the Polish-Bolshevik war he completed the course of commanders of infantry regiments and division infantry. During the May coup, he took the side of Józef Piłsudski. At the age of 35, he was promoted to brigade general with seniority and was then the youngest general in the Polish Army. When World War II broke out, Smorawinski was the commander of Corpus No. 2 District in Lublin, where he organized the mobilization of troops and supplies bases for fighting troops. Then he went to Kowl and later to Włodzimierz Wołyński. After the Red Army entered the territory of Eastern Poland gen. Smorawinski forbade his subordinate troops from attacking the enemy and started negotiations. According to the agreement, he was to go with his unit towards Bug, but after leaving on 20 September 1939 he was detained as a prisoner of war. Then he stayed in POW camps for several months and was placed in a camp in Kozielsk. A year later, in April, he was murdered along with the rest of the victims declared enemies of Soviet power in Katyn. He was one of two identified generals during the exhumation of a corpse in the Katyn Forest in 1943. For his service to the country he was awarded, among others. The Silver Cross of the Order of War of Virtuti Militari, the Commander's Cross of the Order of Rebirth of Poland, the Cross of Independence, four times the Cross of Valiant and the Golden Cross of Merit. In 2007, he was posthumously promoted to the rank of Maj-Gen by the deceased. President of the Republic of Poland Lech Kaczyński. Thank you.

Several bottles of gasoline were thrown in from a crowd gathered under KW PZPR's building, which started a fire. The balance of the first day of December events in Szczecin is 13 hours of protests, assaults, fights and demonstrations, from hours. 9 a.m. at Szczecin Shipyard until 9 a.m. 22. 13 people died in the riots. The next day, on December 18th, a general occupation strike began at the Szczecin plant. At the gate of Szczecin Shipyard there was a military which, as a result of bilateral provocations, opened fire to demonstrators and killed two people and injured several others. On 19 December, the city was taken over by a strike committee of more than 110 different workplaces, headed by Mirosław Dopierała. Demonstrators demanded that the authorities comply with 21 demands, among others. the establishment of independent trade unions, the punishment of the guilty massacre, the increase in earnings by 30% and the abolition of the increase in food prices. The strike ended December 22. During the December revolt, the PZPR Provincial Committee was seized and set on fire, attempts were made to take over the command of the voivodship MO and the seat of the voivodship commission of trade unions, the office of the prosecutor's office, the arrest of the investigators, the military unit and the municipal building of the national council. The events led to the resignation of Władysław Gomulka from the position of First Secretary.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u930	Declaration by Members on the amendment of the Act on the Prohibition of the Promotion of Communism or other totalitarian system by the names of buildings, objects and public utility devices (prints Nos 985 and 1080) The draft law amending the Law on the Prohibition of the Promotion of Communism or other totalitarian system by the names of buildings, objects and public utility devices extends its scope. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 1 paragraph 3 by clarifying the concept of another totalitarian system, it extends it to such categories as: Prussian, Russian and German militarism, Ukrainian and Lithuanian nationalism, which is incompatible with scientific definitions. One of the professors at the University of Warsaw said in this regard: "The law contains nonsense: most nationalisms were not totalitarian, just as totalitarian was not Prussian or Russian militarism. I am concerned about the possibility of interpreting regulations and, consequently, any qualification of commemorative objects for liquidation. I fear that the introduction of such records could cause mass destruction of monuments and commemorative plaques, particularly concerning the environments of national and ethnic minorities, for which these sites are an important element of culture, history and identity. The proposed amendment to the Law on the Prohibition of the Promotion of Communism may also result in action against commemorations which have been restored at the initiative of persons belonging to national and ethnic minorities since 1990. To a large extent, it can refer to monuments and commemorations related to the history and national-cultural identity of minorities, especially German, Ukrainian, Lithuanian, Belarusian and Slovak. It should be remembered that the vast majority of commemorations have been restored as a result of long-term arrangements by local authorities, voivodships, the Council for the Protection of the Remembrance of the Fight and Martyrdom and minority representatives. The Seym Committee on National and Ethnic Minorities was also involved in the negotiations. As a result of these arrangements, a compromise has been reached that has allowed the majority of such monuments and other objects to be organized. This process was particularly evident in Opole, where for several years there was a committee in charge of the voivodeship. monuments. It consisted of representatives of local government, voivodeship, the Church and German minority. The effect of the committee is to regulate the issue of commemoration and end the so-called. A monumental war. Today, in the face of the proposed provisions of the amendment of the law, one should be aware of the risk of creating another monumental war, which can also cause retorts in neighbouring countries, where Polish monuments are located. I therefore appeal to the Law and Justice Club to take into account my concerns - confirmed by the opinions of many authorities, among others. Prof. Grzegorz Janusz, Prof. Piotr Madajczyk and Prof. Krzysztof Ruchniewicz - and decided to withdraw from the legislative work the draft amendment to the Act on the prohibition of the promotion of communism or other totalitarian system (prints Nos 985 and 1080) or to leave the existing definition of totalitarianism recognised by the scientific community and in force in the current legal state.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u931	Statement on air quality in Poland in connection with the latest report of the European Environment Agency. On the website of the European Environment Agency we can find the latest edition of the air status report. It is worrying for us that Poland, together with other Central European countries, is once again at the forefront of this report. According to this latest report, we are still in the forefront of countries that have a problem especially with suspended dust arising mainly from the combustion of solid fuels. In the case of PM10, the most days exceeding the standard are recorded in Bulgaria. Then there is Poland and then Croatia and Greece. Among European countries where there is virtually no PM10 dust problem are Ireland, Estonia, Finland and the United Kingdom. Central European countries are also at the forefront of PM2.5 dust pollution. In our part Europe is also experiencing the highest exceedance for benzo (a) pyrene, which is a very strong carcinogen resulting, inter alia. when burning mainly plastic in unsuitable installations. Unfortunately, Poland is the leader in this category. Poor air quality in Poland is mainly responsible for low emissions, i.e. burning poor fuel quality and garbage in domestic furnaces. The latest report shows that air quality in Europe has improved significantly. However, many inhabitants of cities, especially in Poland and the whole of Central Europe, are still exposed to higher levels of pollution. Contaminants are both directly harmful to man and to the environment. In its report, the European Environment Agency points out the need for a thorough analysis of the causes of pollutant emissions and changes not only in the economy but also in the way of life. I am deeply aware that improving this situation is a very difficult task. However, I would like to ask the High House and all ministers and representatives of the ministries to take action to reduce air pollution in our country. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u932	Statement on plans to change the name of three Tarnów streets to Lech Kaczyńskiego Avenue. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Some time ago, members of the Tarnów Law Association proposed to change the name of the streets of Elektra, Spokojna and Błona to Lech Kaczyńskiego Avenue. Of course, the idea gained fierce groups of both opponents and supporters. This initiative immediately divided Tarnów. Almost 2,000. Tarnow's inhabitants signed a petition against changing the name of the streets. Neither party should be criticised, because the essence of democracy is precisely that everyone can take a specific position on a given matter. However, such a change will cause a lot of normative problems, as well as financial problems for entrepreneurs who are based on this network of streets. There are over 70 such facilities. Company owners estimate costs at even several thousand zlotys. A copy of the documents that will have to be mentioned are, among others. registration of cars, documents from tax offices or ZUS and documents related to KRS. Entrepreneurs will also be forced to update their promotional and information materials, such as: commercials on cars. This problem also applies to natural persons living on this street. The exchange will be subject to driver's license or passports and other personal documents. To sum up, it will certainly not be a small cost. It is worth noting at this point that in no case do we oppose the name proposed, but changes that bring very high costs and administrative burdens. Ladies and gentlemen! Unfortunately, this matter divided the Tarnów society. However, it turns out that it is possible to take a completely different path in this respect, without having to bear the administrative costs of the proposed changes, and this solution is absolutely unacceptable in the name of the sleepers. Lech Kaczyński, but on the contrary. Well, it's a great place to name a sleeper. President Lech Kaczyński, there is a roundabout located next to Tarnów nitrogen plants. This place is also representative and could properly serve as a memorial of the memory of the dead. The president. Interestingly, it was this route - at that time there was still a crossroad - that President Lech Kaczyński moved towards Wierzchosławic after visiting Tarnów. In this case, it would also take an extremely transcendent and symbolic dimension to follow our proposal. In our view, consideration should be given seriously to abandoning the original shape of the planned changes. Citizens will avoid many problems of a formal and financial nature, and the name of the dead. Lech Kaczyński will be commemorated with dignity.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-14-sejm-32-2.u933	Declaration on the ceremony in Lubartów celebrating the 35th anniversary of the martial law. Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, 13 December 1981 is one of the most tragic dates in the recent history of Poland. Communist authorities declared war on the nation resulting in many victims, repressions and dramatic consequences for the country. We do not forget about this date, which is reflected in the celebrations organized throughout Poland. On Sunday 11 December, the 35th anniversary of the martial law was celebrated in Lubartów. The celebrations began with Holy Mass. for the homeland in the Basilica of St. Anna, attended by representatives of the municipal authorities, headed by Mayor Janusz Bodziacki and his deputy Radosław Szumcem. The celebrations began with Holy Mass. for the homeland in the Basilica of St. Anna, sent away by Fr. Andrzej Jóźka. It was attended by banner mails, veterans, representatives of municipal authorities headed by Mayor Janusz Bodziacki and his deputy Radosław Szumcem, as well as councillors, scouts, regionalists, school heads, teachers and students. The performances of the Lubartowska choir under the direction of Alfred Kędzior and young people from the 2nd High School of General Education. Peter Firley. After the academy in the Lubartowski Gallery, the Cultural Centre was also able to see the exhibition "Solidarity" from the materials of the Institute of National Remembrance.
